## 2026-11-17 · chat · lists: fixing a closed list in place (L6b-1)

- On chat/lists-fix on main (fa016e7: L6a merged as #181 and migrated, then the nightly's #182). Orel chose on 16 November 2026: each item's history first, then fixing a closed list, then a switch for a role. This is the L6 plan's L6b-1, for those who run the lists (lists.manage); L6b-2 (the key lists.fix, the owners' window fixHours, the switch at view) is the next PR, and `private.can_fix_list` is the one place it widens. Defaults on Orel's open questions, applied until he answers at the end: a fix keeps the list closed and changes items in place (Q1: who closed it and when, its lateness and the lists.finish row stay; no lists.sent; nobody is told); the history keeps earlier text (Q3). Missed and cancelled lists are never fixed; a required item is changed but never emptied.
- Migration `20261116000300_lists_fix.sql`. Three columns: `list_item_changes.fixed` (not null, default false), `list_items.fixed_at` and `lists.fixed_at`; an item or a list reads as fixed while its `fixed_at` is after the list's `finished_at`, so a reopen and a new finish clear the mark without touching the column. `private.record_item_change` replaced (same parameters) to mark a change to a finished list. `private.can_fix_list(l, actor, at_time)`: a finished list and lists.manage, for now. `private.write_list_item` and `write_list_item_photo`: L6a's bodies of `set_list_item` and `set_list_item_photo`, moved, with a `fixing` flag; not fixing, every rule as before. Fixing: first what filling asks, holding the list or running the lists (42501), so nobody else learns from a fix whether a list is finished; then 55000 unless finished ('Only a finished list is fixed'), 42501 unless `can_fix_list`, 23514 with hint 'required' for a required item a fix would empty (a required photo is replaced, never removed); `done_by` and `done_at` stay while the item stays done (readings over time count by `done_at`), one a fix makes done is the fixer's; `fixed_at` on the item and the list; one audit row `lists.fix` {list, name, item, label, kind, unit, changed, from, to of checked, given, reading}, no text; `lists.out_of_range` still logs and tells; a fix that changes nothing writes nothing. `public.set_list_item` and `set_list_item_photo` are now sql wrappers with the same signatures and grants; new `public.fix_list_item` and `fix_list_item_photo`, the service role's alone. `notify_for_lists` has no arm for `lists.fix`, so nobody is told; `private.log_perm` already places `lists.`. Types written by hand as db:types would (the three columns, the two functions).
- App, `@db`: `@db/list-failures` (new, tested): `listFailureOf` as before, and `itemFailureOf(error, fixing)`: 23514 with hint 'required' is "keepRequired", a fix's 55000 is "reopened", a missing function stays "notYet". `setListItem(actor, item, result, {fixing, at})` calls `fix_list_item` when fixing; `setListItemPhoto` and `removeListItemPhoto` take `fixing` and point through `fix_list_item_photo`. The new columns are read beside the rest so the app works before Migrate: `lists.fixed_at` through its own small query (`fixedTimesOf`, as `namedRolesOf`), `list_items.fixed_at` through `itemRows`' fallback and `getListReadings`' own, `list_item_changes.fixed` through a second read without it (`CHANGE_COLUMNS_BEFORE_FIX`, a row without it reads as not fixed). `ListSummary.fixedAt`, `ListItem.fixedAt`, `ListItemChange.fixed`, `ListReading.fixedAt`, `DayReading.fixed`.
- `@domain/lists`: `fixedAfterClose(fixedAt, list)`; history entries carry `fixed`, and a fix never folds with a change before it; `itemLine(item, entries, list)` gains the kind "fixed" (who fixed it last and when, while the list reads as fixed); `withItemChange` takes `fixed`; `fixRuns(changes)`: one timeline line per person's fixes within `FIX_RUN_MINUTES` = 10, with how many items. `@domain/activity`: `lists.fix` (type, lenient schema, parse; not routine), folded per actor and list within `FOLD_MINUTES`, each item once from where it began to where it ended, and a value put back where it began says nothing.
- List screen: the server actions take `fixing`; `currentList` gives `can.fix` (finished, lists.manage, never a view session). A finished list shows "תיקון הרשימה" (secondary, a pencil) in the header where "פתיחה מחדש" was, never while the finisher's five-minute undo shows (lists.spec and design.spec's undo assertions hold; a cancelled list keeps "פתיחה מחדש"). Fix mode is the screen's own state: the accent card "מתקנים רשימה שנסגרה" under the header, saying who closed it and when, with "פתיחה מחדש לכולם" (the existing reopen) and its hint inside for whoever runs the lists; every change saves at once as a fix (a required tick taken back, or a required reading or answer emptied, stays as it was and is refused on the screen with "keepRequired" before it is sent, which names the way to have it done again, "פתיחה מחדש לכולם"; the card says so too; a required photo has no remove); passing and finishing are gone; "סיום התיקון" is the one main action, in the dock below the desktop and in the header on it, saves what is still typed (a count still waiting, a number typed but not left) and waits for every save on its way: when one is refused the screen stays where the failure's words put it (still fixing, or filled now), otherwise it leaves fix mode with the keyboard back on "תיקון הרשימה" and reads the list again, with "התיקון נשמר"; while a photo saves the dock says why it waits ("photoStillSaving"), as filling does. The marks: "תוקן אחרי הסגירה · שירן · 14:20" on the item's line (a history row that opens the sheet), the Badge "אחרי הסגירה" on its fix rows in "מה קרה בפריט", " · תוקנה ב-14:20" after who closed it in the hero and on ListCard and ListTable on /lists and /lists/today (`closedWords`, the date when the fix wasn't on the day it closed), "פריט אחד תוקן אחרי הסגירה, על ידי שירן, ב-14:20" on the timeline, and " · תוקן" in the day's readings on /lists/today and the desktop readings table, and "תוקן" under the day's reading in the week's readings there. Activity log: "תיקון ברשימה {name} אחרי שנסגרה: {label}" or "{N} תיקונים ברשימה {name} אחרי שנסגרה", each value before and after as hours corrected show them ("קריאה: 3 °C, במקום 30 °C"), an answer, a comment or a photo only that it changed. Roles: editHint.lists ends "…רואים את כל הרשימות ומתקנים אותן גם אחרי שנסגרו."
- Twins: `/design/shell/lists/list-kitchen-opening?state=fix` (Noa closed it at 08:05 with −12°C on the freezer; Shiran fixing it), `?state=fixed` (fixed to −19°C at 08:10) and `/design/shell/lists/today?state=fixed`; the weekly cleaning now shows "תיקון הרשימה"; two `lists.fix` rows in the activity demo. Words in `app.lists.list.fix`, `list.timeline.fixed`, `list.readings.fixed`, `list.failed.keepRequired` and `reopened`, `card.fixed` and `fixedOn`, `app.manage.activity.actions.lists.fix…`, he and en twins.
- Tests: pgTAP `lists_fix.test.sql` (47, ids b2…, numbers 050-053-xxxx, times on 10 December 2026); unit `packages/domain/lists.test.ts` (the mark, folding, the line, the screen's own fix, the timeline's runs), `packages/domain/activity.test.ts` (parse and fold), `packages/db/list-failures.test.ts`, `packages/db/list-history.test.ts` (`fixed`, and rows without it) and `app/(app)/lists/list-words.test.ts` (`closedWords` with "תוקנה"). e2e: design.spec lists the three twins and drives the fix twin (a required reading refused empty, a fix saved, "התיקון נשמר", the marks, the day), and the new `e2e/lists-fix.spec.ts` at 390, 820 and 1440 (with `finishDemoList`, `backdateDemoListFinish`, `fixDemoListItem`, `reopenDemoList` and `demoListClose` in `e2e/db.ts`): Noa closes the kitchen's opening with 30°C, ten minutes back; Shiran fixes it to 3°C; the close stays Noa's, one fixed row and one log row, no line to anyone, the marks on the item, the sheet, the hero, Today and the activity log; Noa reads the marks with no button; a list opened again meanwhile says so and is filled.
- Decided here, beyond the plan: the toasts that outlast the screen ("התיקון נשמר", and why a change didn't save when the list closed, opened again or is no longer theirs) live in a frame around the keyed screen (`ListScreenFrame`), since the key now changes with `fixedAt`; the activity words show each value as hours corrected do (the plan's `fixChange` word wasn't needed); the readings table isn't editable in fix mode, since the item above it is, and two fields of one name would confuse; entering fix mode puts the focus on the card's title, and leaving it puts it back on "תיקון הרשימה", also on the screen read again after a fix saved (the frame carries it); a required tick is a real checkbox in fix mode, so its state is announced and a tap says why it stays.
- The nightly's open item from #182: at 390 a history line that wraps broke inside "עוד שינוי אחד", its chevron apart from the words, and a fix's line ("תוקן אחרי הסגירה · …") is longer still. In `@ui/checklist-item` the chevron is now inline at the end of the words, "· עוד N שינויים" and the chevron wrap whole (`whitespace-nowrap`), and a word joiner holds the chevron to the last word when there is no count.
- Checked locally: `biome check .`, `next typegen`, `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (149 files, 2453 tests), `pnpm build`, and the twins by eye at 390, 820 and 1440 in light and dark (the closed list's "תיקון הרשימה", fix mode, the fixed list and its sheet, Today), with no console errors and no sideways scroll. Not run here: the migration, pgTAP and plpgsql_check (no database) and Playwright; CI runs them.
- Demo, after merging and migrating, in the demo restaurant at 390, 820 and 1440 (its people: Ori, Shiran, Noa, Yoav, Galit; the kitchen's lists are Noa's, and Shiran runs the lists): Noa fills this morning's "פתיחת מטבח", writes 30°C on "מקרר ראשי" by mistake and closes it. The fridge takes 0 to 5°C, so her 30°C sends Shiran and Ori the line "מחוץ לטווח", as any reading out of its range does, and Shiran can open the list from it (or from "היום") more than five minutes later. She presses "תיקון הרשימה", changes the fridge to 3°C and presses "סיום התיקון". The list still reads "נסגרה ב-… על ידי נועה כהן · תוקנה ב-…", the item "תוקן אחרי הסגירה · שירן הדגמה · …" with the earlier 30°C in its sheet. Noa sees the same marks and no button; "יומן פעולות" shows "תיקון ברשימה פתיחת מטבח אחרי שנסגרה: מקרר ראשי" with "קריאה: 3 °C, במקום 30 °C".

## 2026-11-16 · chat · lists: each item's history (L6a)

- On chat/lists-item-history on main (cb7118a). Orel chose on 16 November 2026 to build each list item's history first: who ticked, who changed what, and when. The L6 plan's L6a; fixing a closed list (L6b-1, then the switch L6b-2) comes after it, and nothing here blocks it. Defaults on Orel's open questions, applied until he answers at the end: the history keeps the earlier text of answers and comments; earlier photos are not kept; everyone who reads the list reads its items' history; nobody is notified of history.
- Migration `20261116000200_list_history.sql`. The key `list_items_id_list_business_key` (`id`, `list_id`, `business_id`). `public.list_item_changes`: an identity id, a composite foreign key to that key (on delete cascade), `changed_by` (on delete set null), `changed_at`, `changed` (which of checked, given, reading, answer, comment, photo; at least one), and the item after the change under list_items' own checks, `photo` and `done` as booleans; three indexes. `public.list_reopens`: the close a reopen undid (`was`, `was_by`, `was_at`) and who reopened when, to `lists (id, business_id)` with cascade; three indexes. RLS on both, "changes follow their list" and "reopens follow their list" (whoever reads the list), select only for authenticated, and insert, update, delete and truncate revoked from the service role. `private.refuse_history_change()` before update, its arguments naming the person columns, lets through only a person going to null with nothing else changed (an account deleted). `private.record_item_change(item_before, item_after, actor, at_time)`; `public.set_list_item`, `set_list_item_photo` and `reopen_list` replaced from their latest bodies with only this change and their grants restated; `private.backfill_list_item_changes()`, run once.
- `done_by` changes meaning: a save that changes nothing writes nothing, and who did it and when move only with the item's value (checked, given, reading, answer). A comment alone, or the same value again, no longer hands the item to whoever saved it; clearing it nulls them; a photo item keeps them. On an open list, "מי" in the readings means who gave the reading. The list screen's own state does the same.
- App: `@db/lists` `getList` reads the newest 900 rows of `list_item_changes` (by `changed_at`, `id`) and of `list_reopens` (by `reopened_at`, `id`), put oldest first, under RLS beside the rest (the API answers at most 1000 rows and would drop the newest, so a list changed more than that loses only its oldest), each row checked with zod in the new `@db/list-history`, and a missing table reads as no history, outside the loop that says "notYet", so the list opens before Migrate. `ListDetail` gains `changes` and `reopens`.
- `@domain/lists`: `ITEM_FIELDS`, `ListItemChange`, `ListReopen`; `itemHistory(item, changes)` (entries oldest first, each part from the change before it, the first from an item as it is made; one person's changes within `HISTORY_FOLD_MINUTES` = 2 of each other fold; a run that ends where it began is dropped when it leaves the item undone or touched only its comment, and kept when it leaves it done with the value it had (someone else's tick taken back and given again, a reading changed and put back), saying that value, since it made the item theirs (`done_by`); an item done with no changes is one entry from `doneBy`, `doneAt`, as the backfill writes it, `doneChangeOf`); `withItemChange` (the screen's own change, as the database writes it); `itemLine` (who did it and when, or what changed last, and how many more); `listTimeline(list, reopens)` (each close a reopen undid and the reopen, then the close now; a reopen by its own closer within `REOPEN_MINUTES` is the undo and neither line shows) and `byTime`.
- `@ui/checklist-item`: `history {line, more, label, onOpen}`, a quiet 44px row of its own under the item, outside the check's `<label>` and at `ps-14` past the circle, a chevron mirrored for RTL, named for screen readers with the item ('מה קרה בפריט "…"', which reads with any label) before its words, which also describe the checkbox; in place of `doneLine`, and there read-only too. `/design` shows it ticked with more changes, taken back, and read-only, each as its story left it (they don't tick there), with the sheet it opens, in Hebrew and English.
- List screen: the line under an item reads "{name} · 07:42" for an item done once (as before), "· עוד N שינויים" after it with more, and "{what} · {name} · {time}" for one not done that changed ("הסימון הוסר · איתי · 08:08"), with the date when it isn't the list's day. It opens the sheet "מה קרה בפריט": the item's name under the title, one row a change on `bg-elevated`, what happened (a reading from and to, an answer or a comment written, changed with its text, or removed, a photo added, replaced or removed) over who and when, and "תמונות קודמות לא נשמרות." when a photo was replaced or removed; it only reads. A tick, a count, a reading, an answer or a comment made here joins the story at once and leaves it if it didn't save; a photo joins once it is saved; each carries the server's time (the device's offset to `now`, measured as the screen opens), so a phone whose clock is off, or the demo's fixed morning, reads as the database will write it. On desktop the readings table's "מי" opens the same sheet whenever the item's line does, with who read it, or the line's own words once the reading was cleared ("הקריאה נמחקה · …"), and "מתי" carries the date as the line does. Someone no longer on the team reads "מי שכבר לא בצוות" (`app.lists.former`) on the lines, in the sheet and in the table; nobody named (an account deleted) reads "בלי שם". The comment sheet says under its field that the comment stays in "מה קרה בפריט" even once changed or removed (`comment.kept`). The timeline ("מה קרה ברשימה") puts every entry in time order, with the date on a time that isn't on the list's day, and shows earlier closes with "נפתחה מחדש על ידי…" or "הוחזרה על ידי… אחרי שבוטלה". Phone: the line under each item, the sheet from below, the timeline folded at the end; tablet the same in the items column with the timeline beside; desktop also the table.
- Words in `app.lists.list.history` (and `doneByOn`, `comment.kept`, `timeline.reopened`, `timeline.restored`, `app.lists.former`, `design.checklistOther`, `design.checklistComment`), he and en twins. Twins: `/design/shell/lists/list-kitchen-opening?state=history` (Noa wrote 4°C on the fridge at 07:42, Itay changed it to 6°C at 08:02 and added a note at 08:05; a note on the preparations; Omer's tick of the surfaces taken back by Itay at 08:08) and `/design/shell/lists/list-weekly-cleaning?state=history` (Noa closed it at 07:16, Shiran opened it again at 07:17, Itay closed it at 07:20), both in design.spec.ts' list.
- Tests: pgTAP `list_history.test.sql` (54, ids b1…, numbers 050-052-xxxx, times pinned to 3 December 2026; an order's tick names its count, the same tick again writes nothing, a count changed is a row of its own) and both tables in `rls_enabled.test.sql`; unit `packages/domain/lists.test.ts` (each kind's parts, folding and the dropped run, a run that does the item again kept, the done fallback and the backfill's unnamed comment, the screen's own change, the line, the timeline's undo, `byTime`) and `packages/db/list-history.test.ts` (rows read, an unknown field or a bad row left out). e2e: design.spec.ts opens the fridge's story on the twin at 390, 820 and 1440 in both appearances (the line's words, the sheet's three rows, axe and overflow with it open, and the readings table's "מי" on desktop), and the new `e2e/lists-history.spec.ts` (with `demoListItem` and `setDemoListItem` in `e2e/db.ts`): Noa writes 4°C, Shiran's 6°C through the database names her with "עוד שינוי אחד" and the sheet lists both, Noa's comment keeps the item Shiran's ("עוד 2 שינויים", also read again), a tick Shiran takes back and gives again is hers with Noa's kept, and the team viewing as a manager reads the story.
- Decided here, beyond the plan: the twins use the demo's own people and morning (Itay for Dana and Dani, 08:02 for 11:02), since Dana is only invited there and the demo's clock is 08:12; the line says a comment as "נוספה הערה" or "ההערה שונתה" (`history.commentAdded`, `commentEdited`), not its text, which the item shows right above it, while the sheet keeps the text; a line that can only say a written reading or answer (an item not done can't end on one) falls back to who and when; the line and the sheet carry the date when a change isn't on the list's day (`doneByOn`, `history.lineOn`), so the done line of a list that runs past midnight has it too. From the database part: `record_item_change`'s parameters are `item_before` and `item_after` (L6b's create-or-replace keeps them), the backfill is a function so pgTAP can run it, and `reopen_list` takes the close from `finished_*` or `closed_*` by the status.
- Checked locally: `biome check .`, `next typegen`, `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` on `packages`, `modules`, `tooling` and `app` (all green: 147 files, 2433 tests). Not run here: the migration, pgTAP and plpgsql_check (no database), `pnpm build`, Playwright (design.spec.ts' new twins; the plan's `e2e/lists-history.spec.ts` isn't written yet), and the screens by eye at 390, 820 and 1440.
- Demo, after merging and migrating, in the demo restaurant at 390, 820 and 1440 (its people: Ori, Shiran, Noa, Yoav, Galit; the kitchen is Noa's, and Shiran runs the lists): Noa writes 4°C on "מקרר ראשי" in the kitchen's opening list; Shiran changes it to 6°C; Noa adds a note. Everyone who sees the list reads "שירן הדגמה · {time} · עוד 2 שינויים", and the sheet lists all three with names and times. One person's changes within 2 minutes read as one, so if Shiran writes the note herself she waits more than 2 minutes first, or the line reads "עוד שינוי אחד" and the sheet two rows. Shiran takes back one of Noa's ticks: "הסימון הוסר · שירן הדגמה · {time} · עוד שינוי אחד"; ticked again at once, "שירן הדגמה · {time} · עוד שינוי אחד", the sheet keeping Noa's tick. Shiran reopens the list once Noa finished it, and Noa finishes it again: the timeline shows Noa's close, the reopen and the new close.
- Next: L6b-1, fixing a closed list in place for those who run the lists (`fix_list_item`, the mark "תוקן אחרי הסגירה"), then L6b-2, the switch "תיקון רשימות שנסגרו". Still open from L6a: Orel's answers to the plan's three questions (and, with Q3, whether the comment sheet's line `comment.kept` stays).

## 2026-11-16 · chat · lists: someone taken out of a department keeps no line of its lists (R11d's open item)

- On chat/lists-department-lines on main (6b4243c, R0–R11e merged). R11d's "Still open": someone taken out of a department while still on the staff kept the `list:`, `list.due:` and `list.late:` lines of its lists, and the `list.finished:` line of an order it asked for (`private.notify_for_lists` sends it to the sender and the asking department's people), unread in the inbox and, with a push still queued, on the way to their phone with the list's name, though the list is no longer theirs. R11d closed the same gap for someone moved off a named role, inside `public.set_member_role`.
- Migration `20261116000100_lists_department_lines.sql`, replacing nothing. `private.settle_former_list_lines(business, person, at_time)` (sql, security definer, empty search path): for someone active on the staff, their unresolved lists-module lines of the lists they are no longer among are read (if not already), resolved and their queued push skipped, as `public.pass_list` settles a list's former holders: the `list:`, `list.due:` and `list.late:` lines once they no longer hold the list (`private.holds_list`), and the `list.finished:` line once they neither sent the list nor are in the department that asked for it. `private.settle_lines_off_department()` and the trigger `department_members_settle_lines` (after delete, or an update of `department_id` or `user_id`, for each row) call it for the row's old person at `now()`; a business being deleted (the demo reset) is skipped, and a failure is a warning, as the log's triggers' are.
- By holding, not by seeing (review finding): R11d's block asks `private.sees_list`, so whoever runs the lists (`lists.manage`, every manager by default) kept the lines, since they see every list. Orel's decision has no such exception ("מי שהוציאו ממחלקה כבר לא רואה התראות שמחכות לו על הרשימות שלה", Orel, 16 November 2026, issue #1), `pass_list` settles every former holder, managers too, and the Lists tab (`public.my_open_lists`, by `holds_list`) stopped counting the list while the bell kept it. So a manager taken out of the bar has the bar's lines settled like anyone; they still see the list, and if it runs late after, they get `list.late_team:` (which leaves out only holders). Taken by the recommendation, reversible: if Orel wants managers to keep them, the helper's first branch asks `sees_list` again.
- Why a row trigger: one place covers every writer (`set_department_members`; `leave_staff`, from `remove_member` and `set_member_role`; `claim_invite` as an accountant) and any later one, without replacing a function. `set_department_members` deletes only the people not kept (`dm.user_id <> all (wanted)`) and inserts the kept ones with `on conflict do nothing`, so a re-save deletes nobody and nobody kept is deleted and put back; an after row trigger fires at the end of the delete statement, once all its rows are gone. Not a deferred constraint trigger (nothing takes someone out and puts them back in one transaction, it would wait past every check in the same transaction, the pgTAP files' among them, and no trigger here is one); not a statement trigger with a transition table (a change takes out a few people at most; none here uses one).
- Leaving the staff is as before: every such path makes the membership inactive (`remove_member`) or an accountant's (`set_member_role`, `claim_invite`) before it deletes the person's department rows, so the helper settles nothing of theirs and their lines are put away by the caller as before (read and off the phone, not resolved). A department removed on screen is archived and keeps its people, so nothing changes there; a business's deletion takes its lines with it.
- Tests: pgTAP `lists_department_lines.test.sql` (12, ids d5…, numbers 050-088-xxxx; every time passed explicitly on 1 December 2026, and the due and late lines from the log rows the minute job writes, so nothing reads the real date): the setup's lines, unread and queued; a re-save of the same people through `set_department_members` (as the service role) changes no line, logs nothing and keeps everyone; someone removed from the team and someone made an accountant have the kitchen's lines read and skipped, not resolved; taken out of the kitchen, a worker has its sent, due and late lines read, resolved and skipped, and keeps the lines of a list to them and of the workers' list; a worker in the kitchen and the bar who leaves the kitchen keeps the bar's line and the workers'; a manager taken out has the kitchen's lines settled though they still see the list, and whoever stays keeps theirs; so does a worker given `lists.manage`; the kitchen's order to the bar, sent by a kitchen worker and finished by the bar: whoever is taken out of the kitchen has its `list.finished:` line settled, its sender (also taken out) and whoever stays keep theirs; a row moved from the kitchen to the bar settles the kitchen's lines and the order's finished line; the trigger's events, columns and function; the privileges and definer settings.
- Decided here, beyond the brief: the helper checks "active on the staff" itself, so it is safe from any caller (`set_member_role`'s `not leaving` guard would be redundant once it calls it); the trigger covers an update that moves a row too, which nothing does today; the lines settled are those of whoever a list is for (its holders' three, and the finished line for its sender and the asking department), and the ones for whoever runs the lists (`list.late_team:`, `list.range:`) and the owners (`list.late_owner:`) stay as they are; a line read before keeps its read time; a failure warns rather than refusing the change, as every line written from a trigger.
- One way, accepted (review finding): being put back in the department brings none of it back. The list is theirs again, but the lines settled stay settled and the pushes skipped stay skipped (`lists.due` and `lists.late` are written with `realert => false`, so never again); only a line the list writes again (reopened, passed) or for the first time reaches them. On `/manage/departments` each switch saves at once, so a person switched off and straight back on loses those lines, where before the slip cost nothing; as with a role given back after R11d. Not built: bringing them back on insert would have to tell a line settled by leaving from one settled by the list's own course (a due line settled by a finish before a reopen, or by a pass), and the lines keep no such state.
- Checked locally: `biome check .`, `next typegen`, `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run tooling/` (all green, again after the review's fixes: 25 files, 178 tests). Not run here: the migration and every pgTAP file (no database), plpgsql_check (`supabase db lint`), `pnpm build`, Playwright. No app change and no generated types (a private function and a trigger).
- Demo, after merging and migrating: as an owner, send a list to "מטבח"; one of its workers sees it in the bell. Take them out of "מטבח" in "ניהול העסק", "מחלקות": the line no longer waits (read and settled) and no push goes out for it; a list to them, or to the workers, still waits. The same for a manager in it.
- Next: when `set_member_role` is next replaced for something else, its R11d block should call `private.settle_former_list_lines` instead of its own rule by seeing, so a lists manager moved off a named role is settled too, as here. Still open: being put back in a department brings back no settled line (above); and from R11d, read lines stay in the inbox's "earlier" with the list's name, as a passed list's do.

## 2026-11-16 · chat · roles and permissions: who sees pay and a named role (R11e)

- On chat/roles-named-pay over R11d (chat/roles-named-lists, its PR unmerged) on main with R0–R10, R11a, R11b and R11c. App only, no migration. The bug: the database never gives pay through a role the business named (`private.role_gives` refuses a by-owner key whenever `named` is set, 20261115001500_named_roles.sql section 4), so such a person sees pay only by a personal grant, which `public.set_member_permission` allows them; but "מי רואה שכר" (the owner's `/manage/access` and the console's `/admin/businesses/[slug]/access`, one view) decided by the base role alone. Where managers see pay, a shift lead on managers was counted as seeing it, labelled "גם התפקיד נותן גישה", never offered personal access, and a removal of theirs said their role still covered them: all false. The demo can't show it (demo people take no named role); the local seed and e2e can.
- `@domain/payroll-access`: `PayHolderCandidate.namedRole` (`Pick<NamedRole, "id"> | null`); `coveredByRole(person, roles)`, the one rule (a role given pay, held as it is, never a named role), which `payHolderCount`, `canGetPersonalPay` and the view's `roleCovers` all use; `namedOnPayRole(person, roles)`, in (or choosing) a named role on a role given pay, by the role or a role choice's id; `namedRolesBesidePay(people, roles)`, those roles among `people`, each once with its base.
- `@db/payroll`: `readAccessPeople` (the owner's read under RLS and the console's with the secret key) reads `memberships.named_role` and the role's names with `businessRoleNames`, now exported from `@db/team` and taking any `SupabaseClient<Database>` (members and views read `public.business_roles`; the secret key reads it for the console), rows checked with zod as `@db/roles`' `readRolePeople` does; before the column it reads as before and nobody holds one. `AccessPerson` and `AccessPersonRow` gain `namedRole` (`id`, `name`, `nameEn`).
- Screen: a person in a named role goes by "אחראי משמרת (עובד/ת)" (`app.team.namedRole`, with `namedRoleName` for the locale, as `member-changes.tsx` does) in the personal list and in the add menu; and while someone still offered holds a named role on a role that sees pay, lines under the count, in a polite live region that is always there (`empty:sr-only`, so a switch turned on or a removal says them): one per such role, "מי שבתפקיד {role} לא רואה את השעות והשכר דרך המתג של {base, select, manager {המנהלים} other {העובדים}}" (`app.manage.access.namedRoles`), and, where something can be changed, "אפשר לתת להם גישה אישית ב{add}" with the add control's own words (`namedRolesAdd`), each with its English twin.
- Role sheet (`/team/[person]`, `member-changes.tsx`): for an owner where pay access is in use, choosing a named role on a role that sees pay for someone without their own access says, under the choices, "בתפקיד {role} לא רואים את השעות והשכר דרך המתג של … אחרי השינוי אפשר לתת ל{name} גישה אישית במסך מי רואה שכר." (`app.team.changes.namedPayNote`), since the change takes their pay away at once and nothing said so. `MemberChangesData.pay` (`{ roles, personal } | null`) comes from the access screen's own read (`currentAccess`, null for anyone but an owner, or where pay access isn't in use); no link in the sheet, which would leave before the change is made. The twin /design/shell/team/[person] gives the owner the access demo's roles, workers too with `?state=pay`, so choosing the shift lead for Noa shows it.
- Twins: /design/shell/manage/access names Yoav a shift lead (the shell's `demoNamedRole`, as on the team's twins); `?state=named` gives workers pay too, Yoav without his own access, so he is offered, not counted, and the line shows (in design.spec.ts' list). The console's twin people hold none.
- Tests: `payroll-access.test.ts` (someone in a named role counted only by their own access and once, offered where their base sees pay, `coveredByRole` false for them and still true for the base role, `namedOnPayRole` for a person and a role choice, `namedRolesBesidePay` each role once with its base); `packages/db/payroll.test.ts` (the access people with their named role's names, the console's read without photos, and the read before the migration). e2e: roles.spec.ts' R11b test goes on to switch workers' pay on (`setRolesPayRole`) and open `/manage/access`: workers on, the count of one (the owner), the line for the shift lead on workers and the one pointing to "הוספת אדם", and נועה offered as "נועה תפקידים · אחראי משמרת (עובד/ת)", at every size; written from the specs' own selectors, not run here.
- Decided here, beyond the brief: the lines sit under the count in the roles' section, by the switches they qualify, one per role (each by its own name and the switch it isn't under), and only while one of its people is still offered, so they mark what needs doing and go once everyone in it has access; in view-only the pointer to "הוספת אדם" is left out. The role sheet's note is for owners only: they give pay, and a manager who changes a role can't. `businessRoleNames` is reused rather than a second read of `business_roles`.
- Review (six findings): the line reworded to name the role and the switch and to point to "הוספת אדם" (two findings, one wording), shown only while someone in it is offered, and said by a live region; the role sheet's note for a change that takes pay away (above, a note only, the reviewer's recommendation; an automatic personal grant would be Orel's call); the roadmap's R11e sentence says when the line shows, and its stage line ends with what is next.
- Checked locally: `biome check .`, `tsc --noEmit` (with stand-ins for Next's generated `PageProps`/`LayoutProps`, as `next typegen` wasn't run), `vitest run` on the touched tests (all green). Not run here: `pnpm build`, Playwright (roles.spec.ts, design.spec.ts), and the screen by eye at 390, 820 and 1440.
- Demo, after merging, in a real business (a demo one takes no invites, so nobody holds a named role there): as its owner with managers seeing pay, add "אחראי משמרת" built on מנהל on "תפקידים והרשאות", make a manager one from their page on the team, then open "ניהול העסק", "מי רואה שכר": they aren't counted, the lines say why and where, and "הוספת אדם" offers them by the role's name. Before that, choosing "אחראי משמרת" in the manager's role sheet says they'd see pay only by their own access.
- Next: nothing left in the roles plan. Its R12 (the guard) is already done: the SQL guard (`tooling/role-checks.ts`) has checked every migration since R1 and `NOT_YET_MOVED` is empty, and `canSeeTeam`, which R12 would have removed, now reads `team.view` rather than the role. Templates that bring their permissions stay deferred. The open hardening item is R11d's: someone taken out of a department keeps the lines of its lists.

## 2026-11-16 · chat · roles and permissions: lists sent to a named role (R11d)

- On chat/roles-named-lists over R10 (local) on R11b (PR #175, in CI) on main with R0–R9 and R11a. R11c (view-as, `20261115001800`) is built beside it and merges first, so this one is `20261115001900` and touches no view session and no invite form. The roles plan's R11 d with Orel's answers of 15 and 16 November 2026 on #1: owners and the team change permissions; a named role is built on manager or worker.
- Migration `20261115001900_named_roles_lists.sql`. `recipient_named_role` on `lists`, `list_templates` and `list_schedules`: memberships' composite foreign key (business_id, recipient_named_role, recipient_role) to `business_roles` (business_id, id, base), on delete set null (recipient_named_role), a partial index each, a check that only a list "to a role" names one (`recipient_kind is not distinct from 'role'`, null-safe for a template chosen each time), and a trigger each (`private.refuse_archived_recipient_role`: an archived role is 22023 `archived`, its row locked `for key share`, a row that keeps its named role not asked again). A list to a named role is for exactly the active people on the staff holding it; one to the role itself reaches everyone in it as before.
- "To the workers" still includes those holding a named role built on worker: they are workers, it is what every list to the workers did before anyone held a named role, and leaving them out would quietly take lists from people the day they are given a role. The same for the managers.
- Replaced, each from its latest version plus only this change (diffed): `private.holds_list` and `private.list_holders` (20261110000300_payroll_access.sql: a named role's holders, or the whole role); `private.list_recipient` (20261109000600_lists.sql: `namedRole` beside `role`, of the business, live, on that base, only on a role; gains the out column `to_named`, so dropped and created again) and `private.list_schedule_fields` (same file: `for_named`, dropped and created again; neither has a policy or BEGIN ATOMIC dependant); `public.send_list` (same file: the list's named role, and `recipient.named_role`/`recipient.role_name` in its `lists.send` row); `public.save_list_template` (20261115001200_roles_lists.sql: a template's default and each time keep it); `private.lists_tick_business` (same file: a time opens its list to its named role while live, and once archived treats it as nobody there, R5's managers-else-owners rule, never the whole base); `public.pass_list` (20261110001400_lists_pass.sql: cleared with the role, which the new check needs); `public.archive_business_role` (20261115001600_named_roles_give.sql: refused while a live template or a time of one, on or paused, sends to it, 55000 `lists` with the count, after `held`); `public.set_member_role` (the same file: someone moved off a named role and still on the staff has the `list:`, `list.due:` and `list.late:` lines of the lists they no longer see, by `private.sees_list`, settled as `pass_list` settles former holders': read, resolved, push skipped). Callers checked: `list_recipient` by `list_schedule_fields`, `save_list_template`, `send_list`; `list_schedule_fields` by `save_list_template`; `holds_list` by `sees_list`, `set_list_item`, `set_list_item_photo`, `finish_list`, `my_open_lists`, `pass_list`; `list_holders` by `notify_for_lists`; `lists_tick_business` by `lists_tick`; `send_list` by `seed_demo_lists`; `archive_business_role` and `set_member_role` by nothing in the database; all unchanged. `copy_template_lists` and `seed_demo_lists` stay (a global template and the demo name no named role).
- Archiving a role lists go to: refused, not a fall back to the base. A list meant for a shift lead would otherwise reach every worker, unasked, and a paused time switched on again would too. Paused times count for that reason. Open lists already sent to it don't block: nobody active holds the role by then (or the archive is refused for `held`), and whoever runs the lists still reads, passes or cancels them, as a list to a role nobody is in. A template archived first never blocks; restored after its role was archived, it reads as a removed department's: the send screen and the editor say the role was removed and ask for another, its times show as pointing at something gone, a save naming it is refused, and the minute job sends to the managers or owners.
- App. `@domain/lists`: a role recipient takes `namedRole` (a uuid; a global template's seed never), `ListHolder` too; `holdsList` takes the person's `namedRole`; `sendDefaults` (`liveRoles`, and `namedRole` out), `recipientGone`, `scheduleGone` and `SchedulePlaces.roles` know an archived named role; `roleChoiceKey`/`roleRecipientOf` for the pickers. `@db/lists`: `namedRolesOf` reads each list's, template's and time's named role beside the rest (none before the column, so every screen reads as before), `getMyLists` takes the person's, `getListRoles`/`getListRolesForTeam` give the business's roles, archived too, none until `lists.recipient_named_role` exists. `@db/roles`: the refusal `lists`. `@domain/activity`: a `lists.send` to a named role says its name.
- Screens. The send screen offers the live named roles after the built-in roles, each with who holds it ("רק מי שבתפקיד הזה · # אנשים", from `getColleagues`, which now gives each member's `namedRole`), one nobody holds yet saying "עוד אין אף אחד בתפקיד" and taking no press, as a shift nobody is on (and a template to it starting with nothing chosen: `sendDefaults`' `emptyRoles`); its button "שליחה לאחראי משמרת", and a template whose role was removed says so; the template editor and its times' sheet offer them in the role menu with the same "רק מי שבתפקיד הזה"; Lists, a list's screen, Today, the screen of templates, the times and the activity log name a list to one by the role's name ("לאחראי משמרת"); "תפקידים והרשאות" (and the console's) counts the live templates that send lists to each named role (`getRoleListsForOwner`/`getRoleListsForTeam` in `@db/roles`, read with the secret key as the matrix is, each template once, by default or by a time on or paused), so "הסרת התפקיד" is off ahead of time and says why ("רשימה אחת נשלחת לתפקיד הזה... ב„הרשימות של העסק”"), and the database's refusal stays as the backstop in the same words. The console's template screens read the roles with the secret key. /design/shell/lists has the shift's opening, a template, its time and this morning's list to the demo's shift lead (`/design/shell/lists/list-shift-opening`, `templates/t-shift-opening`, `send?template=t-shift-opening`, each `?state=roleGone` where the role can be gone), and the console's roles twin `?state=lists` a role held back by its list alone.
- Tests: pgTAP `roles_named_lists.test.sql` (18, ids d4…, numbers 050-087-xxxx; foreign-key and check refusals compared by their code alone; every time pinned to start on 1 November after the template's restore, which starts them from the real clock, so nothing depends on today's date): a list to a named role reaches exactly its active holders, who alone are told and read it with its sender and the list managers (a floor manager on managers among them); to the workers it reaches the shift and bar leads too, to the managers the floor manager, to the floor managers only them; the log; send, template and time refusals (another business's, archived, another base, a named role on a department, not an id); the tables refusing direct writes; templates and times keeping one and changing it; the tick to a named role, to the workers, and to a restored template's archived role (managers); passing clears it; the archive rule in order (lists 2, held 1, lists 1, lists 1 paused, then archived; an archived template keeps it); the bar lead moved to plain worker has the line of the bar lead's list settled and its push skipped, and keeps the workers' lines; today's recipient read; triggers, keys, privileges and definer settings (`set_member_role` among them). Unit: the schemas, `holdsList`, `recipientGone`, `scheduleGone`/`scheduleProblem`, `sendDefaults` (an empty named role too), the picker keys, the activity line, the `lists` refusal, the roles screen's count of lists per named role and its reader. No e2e: it needs the local stack, which can't run here.
- Decided here, beyond the brief: the JSON key is `namedRole` (the lists' JSON is camelCase); every named-role refusal of `list_recipient` is 22023 (the screens' "invalid", as a removed department's), with hints `archived` and `base`; the `lists.send` log names the role so the activity log says it even after a rename; the archive refusal is its own hint (`lists`) with its own words rather than widening "held"'s; the roles screen counts templates (each once), not templates and times as the refusal's detail does, since it speaks of lists; the three foreign keys stay `on delete set null (recipient_named_role)`, as memberships' and invites': nothing deletes a role (delete is revoked even from the secret key), and `no action` could refuse deleting a business (a demo's reset) by the order its cascades fire in, since a time is deleted with its template, a step after the business's roles; moving someone off a named role settles (resolves) their lines of its lists, as `pass_list` does for former holders, not only reads them; someone leaving the staff, a change that keeps the named role, and a change of base alone are as before; a named role nobody holds is offered but takes no list on the send screen (a list to it would reach nobody and tell nobody), as a shift nobody is on.
- Checked locally: `biome check .`, `next typegen`, `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (all green). Not run here: the migration and every pgTAP file (no database), plpgsql_check, `pnpm build`, Playwright, and the screens by eye at 390, 820 and 1440. database.types.ts is written by hand as the generator writes it (three columns, three relationships).
- Demo, after merging and migrating, in a real business (a demo one takes no invites, so nobody holds a named role there): as its owner, with a shift lead given "אחראי משמרת" (R11b), open "רשימות", "שליחת רשימה", "תפקיד", choose "אחראי משמרת" and "שליחה לאחראי משמרת": only the shift lead sees it on Lists and in the bell; a list to "העובדים" still reaches them too. Make "אחראי משמרת" a template's default, then try to remove the role on "תפקידים והרשאות": it says lists go to it.
- Review (seven findings): fixed the pgTAP time that would have failed from 2 December (restore resets `opens_from` to now), the unread list line kept by someone moved off a named role, the roles screen offering to remove a role lists go to, the send screen's empty named role, the editors' missing hint and the design twin that showed no list to a named role; the `on delete` hardening note answered in the migration (above) rather than changed. Still open: someone taken out of a department keeps the lines of its lists in the same way (`set_department_members` touches no line), as before R11d; and read lines stay in the inbox's "earlier" with the list's name, as a passed list's do.
- Next: R11c's merge first (if `20261115001800` replaces `archive_business_role` or `set_member_role`, this one must be rebased on its version); then R12 (the guard), per the roles plan.

## 2026-11-16 · chat · roles and permissions: viewing the app as a named role (R11c)

- On chat/roles-named-view over R10 (local, `20261115001700`), over R11b (PR #175, in CI) on main with R0–R9 and R11a. The roles plan's R11c with Orel's answers of 15 and 16 November 2026 on #1: owners and the team change permissions, and a role an owner names is built on a manager or a worker. From here the team views a business as one of its named roles from the console, and reads exactly what that role holds.
- Migration `20261115001800_named_roles_view.sql`. `view_sessions.named_role` with memberships' and invites' composite foreign key (business_id, named_role, role) to `business_roles` (business_id, id, base), on delete cascade (a role goes only with its business; a view never falls back to its base), an index, and R11a's `private.refuse_archived_named_role` on the table too (`view_sessions_named_role_live`: 22023 `archived`). The session's role stays its rank, so `private.is_viewing`, `is_viewing_staff` and `viewing_payroll` are unchanged and read a view as a named role as one as its base.
- Replaced, each from its latest version plus only this change (diffed): `public.start_view_session` (from 20261025000100_view_as.sql; gains `view_named uuid default null`, so dropped and created again with its grants, no policy or BEGIN ATOMIC body depending on it: of the business else P0002, live else 22023 `archived`, on `view_role` else 22023 `base`, checked before the open session ends; written on the session; its `view_as.start` row adds `named_role` and `role_name`; still security invoker, the service role's only); `public.my_permissions` (from 20261115000700_roles_catalogue.sql), `private.businesses_holding` (from 20261115000900_roles_scheduling.sql) and `private.viewed_perms` (from 20261115001000_roles_manage.sql), each in place, the view branch's `private.role_holds(business, v.role, k.key)` now with `v.named_role`. Callers checked (every function's latest version, creates and drops in order): `view_sessions` is read by `is_viewing`, `start_view_session`, `end_view_session`, `is_viewing_staff`, `viewing_payroll`, `my_permissions`, `preferences_owner`, `businesses_holding` and `viewed_perms`, and only the last three ask what the role holds; `start_view_session` is called by `@db/view-as` alone (and pgTAP with three arguments, unchanged); `my_permissions` by `@db/session`; `businesses_holding` and `viewed_perms` by R3 to R8's policies and functions, unchanged. No newest function or policy asks a view's rank in a way a named role could widen (tooling/role-checks.ts' newest versions: none but the notifications' fixed one).
- App. `@domain/view-as`: `ViewAsChoice` ({role, namedRole}), `viewAsChoices(named)` (each built-in role followed by the business's named roles on it), `viewAsKey`/`parseViewAsChoice` (a button's value is the built-in role or the named role's id), `VIEW_AS_FAILURES`/`viewAsFailureOf` (`failed`, `roleGone`), `ViewSession.namedRole`. `@db/view-as`: `startViewing(actor, business, choice)` passes `view_named` only when set and returns `roleGone` for 22023 `archived`; `getViewableNamedRoles(business)` (live roles in their order, with the secret key; none until `view_sessions.named_role` exists, none before the table). `@db/session`: the open session's `named_role` (tolerating the missing column) and its names (`namedRoleIn`, under the policy that lets a view read them) into `viewing.namedRole` and `membership.namedRole`, so the banner and the shell's card say it; `perms` already follows it through `my_permissions`. The console's "צפייה באפליקציה" (`/admin/businesses/[slug]`): one button per live named role after its base, "צפייה בתור אחראי משמרת" with "תפקיד של העסק, בנוי על עובד/ת" under it (`admin.setup.viewAs.asNamed`, `namedHint`; its English name in English); the action reads the named roles only for a choice that isn't a built-in role and says a role archived since (`?view=roleGone`, `admin.setup.viewAs.roleGone`). The banner names the role. `@domain/activity` reads the start's `role_name`, so the activity log says "צפייה באפליקציה בתור אחראי משמרת (עובד/ת)".
- Orel's small change (16 November, #1): the team invite form shows the roles only an owner gives, a built-in role that holds pay and a named role holding more than the inviter, disabled in their places with the role sheet's reasons (`app.team.changes.payHint`, `heldHint`), instead of leaving them out (`inviteChoices(viewer, named, ownerOnly)` in `@domain/team`, `InviteChoice.blocked`); with none left to offer, the screen says so as before. The console's add-person form is unchanged.
- Twins: /design/console/businesses/demo-restaurant offers the demo's "אחראי משמרת" to view as; /design/shell/view-as?as=named shows the banner naming it (in screens.spec.ts' list); /design/shell/team/invite?as=editor shows the named role disabled.
- Tests: pgTAP new `roles_named_view.test.sql` (14, ids a3…, numbers 050-083-xxxx): the table's foreign key (columns, target, cascade) and trigger; the privileges and settings; views as the four built-in roles holding what R1's frozen view branch gave and today's call logging as before; the view as a shift lead (its session on workers, its log row naming it, its names read by the team member); refusals by the function (archived, another business's, another base, the owner) leaving the open view, and by the table (the trigger, the foreign key compared by its code); no draft saved through the secret key and no change from the browser; the shift lead and a floor lead on managers holding exactly what someone in each holds; each view's own rules beyond its base (shift lead +schedule.edit, clock lead +clock.edit, office +manage.view,team.view, floor lead −schedule.edit,schedule.publish); what six views read (drafts, the team's hours, the log's role rows, invites), their rank, no pay, and `viewed_perms`/`businesses_holding` agreeing with `my_permissions`. Vitest: view-as (domain and a new `@db/view-as` test), team (`inviteChoices`), activity. e2e view-as.spec.ts: the team views the demo shop as "אחראי משמרת" (added through the database for the test and deleted after), offered after workers, the banner naming it, the builder open and the team not, the log naming it, back to the shop.
- Decided here, beyond the brief: on delete cascade rather than set null for the session's named role, so a view never widens to its base; the base and archive checks inline in `start_view_session` rather than `private.live_named_role`, since it runs as the service role (security invoker, unchanged) and the trigger keeps the locked check; `end_view_session` unchanged (its row says the base, the start's names the role); a view of a role archived while open keeps reading what the role holds until it ends (two hours at most), as the archive changes nothing it holds; `membership.namedRole` is the viewed one while viewing (the shell and /me say it); `?view=roleGone` for a role archived since the page was drawn; the invite form's disabled rows reuse the sheet's words.
- After the reviews: a named role archived since the console page was drawn now says so (`?view=roleGone`) instead of a 404: the action reads the form with `readViewAsChoice` (`@domain/view-as`), which answers `gone` for a uuid not among the live named roles, and the database's 22023 `archived` still covers one archived as the view starts; P0002 stays `failed` (it is also "Unknown business", and a role goes only with its business), and the `ViewAsFailure` doc says archived only. pgTAP starts the shift lead's view and the four refusals as the service role, as the app does (still 14). `ChoiceRow` styles a disabled row itself (no hover or pointer, its title in `text-ink-2`, its circle dimmed, its hint, the reason, at full strength), so the invite form and the role sheet drop their `opacity-60` and the lists' send and pass sheets their `cursor-default`; /design shows a disabled row (`design.choiceOff`, `choiceOffHint`). With nothing to offer, the invite screen says pay only when every role is blocked for pay, else "את התפקידים שאפשר להזמין אליהם מכאן נותנים רק הבעלים" (`app.team.invite.ownerOnlyAny`); /design/shell/team/invite?as=held reaches it (in design.spec.ts' list).
- Checked locally: `biome check .`, `next typegen`, `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (all green). Not run here: the migration and every pgTAP file (no database), `pnpm build`, Playwright (view-as, screens, design and team specs), and the screens by eye at 390, 820 and 1440. database.types.ts is written by hand as the generator writes it.
- Demo, after merging and migrating: as the shop's owner Michal add "אחראי משמרת" on "תפקידים והרשאות" (built on עובד) and give it עריכה in סידור; in the console, open "חנות הדגמה", "צפייה בתור אחראי משמרת": the banner says "צפייה בתור אחראי משמרת · חנות הדגמה", "סידור לשבוע הבא" opens and the team screen doesn't; "סיום הצפייה" goes back. Next: R11d (lists to a named role).

## 2026-11-16 · chat · roles and permissions: telling a role's people, "שונה" and "חזרה להתחלה" (R10)

- On chat/roles-changes over R11a (PR #174) on main with R0–R9; R11b (`20261115001600`) is built beside it and merges first, so this one is `20261115001700` and touches no team writer, invite or `claim_invite`. The roles plan's R10 with Orel's answers of 16 November 2026 on #1 to R9's two questions: when owners change what a role does, its people get a notification in the app only; and each area that differs from where it started says "שונה", with "חזרה להתחלה" per role.
- Migration `20261115001700_roles_changes.sql`. A trigger of its own on the log (`audit_log_notifies_roles`, after insert when `action = 'role.permission'`, the lists' and payroll's pattern, so `private.notify_for` stays as it is) calls `private.notify_role_change(entry)`. Whom: for a named role's row, the active people holding it; for a built-in role's, those in it with no named role and those in a live named role on it with no row of its own for the key, since a named role follows its base wherever it has none (`private.role_gives`), so their rule changed too (a named role with its own row for the key keeps it, and its people hear nothing). Never an owner, an accountant, someone who left, the team or the actor. The line `role.changed`: one a person, a role and an area (dedupe `role.changed:<manager|worker|named role id>:<area>`), facts `{role, name?, area, from, to}` where `from` and `to` are what the role held of the area's settable keys (`private.area_rules`: its rule per key; `private.area_holds`: held with `requires`), `source_module` the area's module, audience worker, the actor the owner or the team member. While unread, the next change of that role and area goes on from the line's `from` (a key it lacks from before the change), so an undo that ends where it began deletes the line and a different change updates it; a line already read keeps its place (its `dedupe_key` cleared, a new column comment says so) and the change starts a line of its own; a change that moves nothing the role holds (a key held for want of what it requires) tells nobody. In the app alone: `private.notify`, then `push_state` 'none' and `push_after` null for what it queued, as the reminders' lines in the app alone; in no group. A demo's people get theirs in the app; with notifications off nobody does.
- Replaced, from its latest version plus only this change (diffed): `private.notification_kind_groups` (20261115000500_lists_owners_late.sql's), with `('role.changed', null)`; same signature, grants kept, revoked again. Callers checked: `private.notification_group` (20261112000100_notification_preferences.sql) and packages/db/notification-preferences.db.test.ts, unchanged. New, all security definer with an empty search path and revoked from public, anon and authenticated: `private.area_rules(business, r, area_key, named)`, `private.area_holds(rules)`, `private.notify_role_change(public.audit_log)`, `private.notify_roles_from_log()` (the trigger, which never blocks the change). No public function, so database.types.ts is unchanged.
- App. `@domain/notifications`: the kind `role.changed` (group null, module null, worker and up, staff only, needsAction false, `href` /notifications, `to: "area"`, new `appOnly`), its line `{ role: base | named, area, change }` read from the facts (anything else, or a line that says nothing changed, is a plain one), `linkFor` to the area's screen (`AREA_HREF`) unless the change took the reader's way there (team or manage at none), `alwaysOnPhone` leaving out a kind in the app alone. `@domain/role-access`: `areaChangeOf` and `changesAnything` (what a change of one area gave and took), `startingAccess` (where a role starts: its rule without a row, with `requires`), `areaChanged`, `changedAreas`, `resetParts` (forward and back), `areasWrites`, `parseRoleAreasChange`, `AreaPart`. `app/(app)/notifications/role-change-words.ts`: the line's words, the inbox's and the phone's (`pushWords`, which never sends one), title "ההרשאות בתפקיד {role} השתנו" and the change in the roles screen's words for the area and its switches ("מעכשיו עורכים בסידור", "מעכשיו בלי פרסום הסידור", with "גם"/"בלי" lists), and "את השינוי עשה צוות ConceptApp" from the console. Roles screen (/manage/roles and the console's, one view): each role carries `start` (`rolesData`: the keys' defaults for managers and workers, the base's rule for a named role; a role added on the screen starts where its base is); an area that differs shows "שונה" (an accent Badge in its card, and on a computer in the role's cell of the area's first row, on the switch's or menu's line so the row stays level), and the role's row in the list (phone, tablet) and its column's head say how many ("{count} תחומים שונו"); while any open area differs the role has "חזרה להתחלה" (a secondary button under its cards with the hint saying what the start is, "להגדרה שאיתה כל עסק מתחיל" or "כמו אצל {base}"; in its column's head on a computer), one write of every such area (`setAreas` → `setRoleAreasAction` / `setTeamRoleAreasAction` → `setRoleAreas`, one `set_role_permissions` call), one toast "{role}: הכול חזר להתחלה" whose "ביטול" writes each area back as it was, the keyboard put on the undo since the control goes: meanwhile it waits on the role's name (the phone's title, the tablet's chosen name, the column's head), where the toast's time running out returns it without moving the page, and a refusal or the undo returns it to the control as it comes back. Words in he/en: `app.notifications.kinds.role.changed.*`, `app.manage.roles.changed`, `changedAreas`, `reset.*`, `done.reset`. The inbox twin has a read line of the kind; the roles twins pass `setAreas` stand-ins, and the shell's shift lead starts as workers, so three of its areas say "שונה".
- Tests: pgTAP new `roles_changes.test.sql` (28, ids c4…, numbers 050-084-xxxx): the functions' privileges and definer settings, the trigger, every settable key requiring only a settable key of its own area, `area_holds`, the kind's group; managers lowered in scheduling tell the manager and the deputy (a named role following managers) and nobody else, with the facts, the actor, the area's module, never queued though both have phones, no send taking them, read by the manager under RLS and not by the owner; an immediate undo leaves none; a switch alone, then a second change, one line from where it began; a line read kept and a new one started while the deputy's unread one goes on, and back where it began the deputy's goes; a named role's change tells only its holder; workers' batch tells workers once and "Bar lead" (its own rule for running the lists, its line read first, so a wrong inclusion would show in where its new line starts) only of sending, which it follows workers in; from the console, the team as actor; an actor in the role (written straight to the log) left out; never an owner, accountant, someone who left or the team; a demo's worker in the app; nothing with notifications off; a switch that moves nothing the role holds (publishing off while building is) logged and telling nobody; a settled line kept like a read one; no line ever for a phone; the rules as left. Changed where meant: `roles_chat.test.sql` leaves `role.changed` out of the lowered manager's counts (its lowering now tells them). Vitest: role-access (start, marks, reset parts and writes, change words' shape, the parser), notifications (the kind, its line, links, actor, `alwaysOnPhone`; the "pay always comes" check skips a kind in the app alone), role-change-words (every sentence, the named and lost names, the phone's words with the team), the owner's action, `rolesData`'s start, `@db/roles` `setRoleAreas`. e2e: roles.spec.ts "שונה" after workers' lists are raised, the worker's in-app line (title and words) and the database's (`rolesLines`: unread, push 'none'), "חזרה להתחלה" clearing mark, rules and line with the undo focused, and its undo bringing all three back; design.spec.ts the shift lead's three marks, the reset and its undo on the twin.
- Decided here, beyond the brief: precisely who for a built-in role (above: also a named role's people where it follows the base, because their permission really changed; never where it has its own rule). The marks compare what the role holds (the drawn level and switches), not its rows: a denial kept under an edit that is off holds nothing either way (`areaWrites` keeps it), so it marks nothing, and "חזרה להתחלה" writes the area to where it starts through `areaWrites`, never refused for what a key requires (writing rows straight to their defaults could be: a named role whose base keeps publishing without building). The line's facts hold what is held, not rows, so its words are exactly the change; a read line is kept rather than replaced, so the inbox keeps what the person already read and an undo of a later change removes only the new line. No group: a group is what someone keeps off a phone, and this line never goes there (`appOnly` keeps it out of "pay always comes"); it leads to the area's screen, or the inbox when the change closed it. The demo gets lines like any business. A named role following its base still shows the base's change on this screen only after a reload, as since R11a; its mark is drawn against the start the screen was drawn with, so the two stay consistent until then.
- Checked locally: `biome check .`, `next typegen`, `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (all green). Not run here: the migration and every pgTAP file (no database), `pnpm build`, Playwright (roles.spec.ts, design.spec.ts), and the screens by eye at 390, 820 and 1440. database.types.ts needs no change (no public function).
- Demo, after merging and migrating: sign in as the shop's owner Michal at /enter, open "ניהול העסק", "תפקידים והרשאות", give workers עריכה in רשימות: the card says "שונה"; in another browser the shop's worker sees "ההרשאות בתפקיד עובד/ת השתנו" with "מעכשיו עורכים ברשימות, עם שליחת רשימות" in the bell, never on the phone. Back as Michal press "חזרה להתחלה": the mark goes, and so does the worker's line; "ביטול" brings both back. Next: R11b's merge, then R11c.

- On chat/roles-named over R9 (PR #173) on main with R0–R8. Nobody holds a named role until R11b gives one, so nothing changes for anyone yet.
- Migration `20261115001500_named_roles.sql`. The primary key of `role_permissions` became a unique index nulls not distinct over (business_id, role, named_role, perm) (critique 27); the only `on conflict (business_id, role, perm)` was `set_role_permission`'s (now the new target) and roles_team.test.sql's; `seed_demo_pay` and the setter's pay branch say `on conflict do nothing` with no target, unchanged.

Callers checked: `role_gives` is called by `holds_perm`, `role_holds` and the matrix; `role_holds` by `team_invite`, `team_invite_link`, `invite_closed`, `my_permissions`, `set_role_permission`, `lists_tick_business`, `set_member_role` (through `role_holds_pay` and `role_holds_beyond`), the matrix, `businesses_holding` and `viewed_perms`, every one with three arguments, so each still asks the built-in role; `set_role_permission` by `set_role_permissions`, `@db/payroll` and e2e/db.ts (five arguments, unchanged); the matrix by `@db/roles`. `public.set_member_role` needed no change: the trigger clears the named role when it changes the base; R11b replaces it anyway, to give one.
- Who holds what: someone in a named role holds by role what its row decides, else its base's rule (the base's row, now the one for no named role, else the key's default roles), with `requires` and personal grants as before. A view session still views the built-in roles (R11c). The setters write a named role's rows against its base's rule (its default), so "back to default" removes its row, and refuse a role archived, of another business (P0002) or on another base (22023, hint `base`); its `role.permission` log row adds `named_role` and `name`.
- New: `public.create_business_role(actor, business, base_role, role_name, role_name_en, copy_from, copy_role)` → its id; `public.rename_business_role(actor, role_id, role_name, role_name_en)`; `public.archive_business_role(actor, role_id)`; `public.restore_business_role(actor, role_id)`; all security definer, search_path '', the service role's only, owners and the team only (`private.check_role_changer`, `check_permission_giver`'s rule), the business locked first as every role writer and the demo reset take it. Refusals: a live name (23505, hint `taken`), a twenty-first live role (54000, hint `limit`), a bad name, base or copy (22023, hints `name`, `name_en`, `base`), anyone else (42501), an unknown role or business (P0002), archiving one held by anyone active (55000, hint `held`, the count as detail). Logged `role.create`, `role.rename`, `role.archive`, `role.restore`, which `private.log_perm` already places (`^role\.` → manage.view) and tooling/log-families.ts passes. A demo business takes them as it takes its rules; its reset deletes them with the business.
- App. `@domain/role-access`: `RoleKey` and `isBaseRole` (a role is `manager`, `worker` or a named role's id), `NamedRole`/`NamedRoleRules`, `RoleMatrix.named` and `addsRoles`, `rulesOf`, `parseNewRole`, `parseRoleRename`, `ROLE_LIMIT`, `namedRoleName`; `parseRoleAreaChange` takes a named role's id. `@db/roles`: the matrix's named rows (tolerating a function without the columns: no named roles, none to add), `setRoleArea` for a named role (its id as `grant_named`, its own defaults), `createBusinessRole`, `renameBusinessRole`, `archiveBusinessRole`, `restoreBusinessRole` (the last three only for a role of the business the screen was drawn for), and `getRolePeopleForOwner`/`getRolePeopleForTeam` (each role's people with their named role, photos for the owner; a missing column reads as nobody in one). `/manage/roles` and the console's page draw the named roles after managers and workers with their counts (0 until R11b), "הוספת תפקיד" opens R0's sheet once the database takes them, and each named role is renamed (a sheet) and archived (refused while held, with the words) from its card, on a computer from its column's head; adding's undo archives it, archiving's restores it, renaming's renames it back. Server actions: the owner from the session first (or the team member), Zod (`parseNewRole`, `parseRoleRename`, a uuid), the built-in roles' names refused, then the secret key. The activity log names a named role in its `role.permission` lines and reads the four new actions. Words in he/en (`app.manage.roles.named`, `renameSheet`, `done.*`, `failure.gone|taken|limit|held`, `app.manage.activity.actions.role`, a `named` branch in `rolePermission`).
- Tests: pgTAP `roles_named.test.sql` (53, ids a1…, numbers 050-081-xxxx): R1's resolver frozen and compared for every membership and role in the database before any named role; the table's and functions' privileges; adding by an owner and the team, copied from the base, the other built-in role and another named role (holding what the source holds, never pay); every refusal; the twenty; the unique index (a named role's row beside its base's, one rule a key, on its base, never pay, the upsert target); holding (its rows, its base's, `requires`, personal pay, `my_permissions`, a view as a worker unchanged, a manager in a named role without the managers' pay); the setters; the matrix; renaming, archiving (held, those who left), restoring; the log and its readers; RLS; everyone without a named role holding what they did before any was added; a change of role leaving it; a demo and its reset. Updated where meant: roles_catalogue, roles_setter and payroll_access (the new signatures in their privilege checks), roles_team (the conflict target), rls_enabled (the table). Vitest: role-access, activity, `@db/roles`, the owner's actions, `rolesData`. e2e: roles.spec.ts adds "אחראי משמרת" copied from עובד, sees it with nobody in it, archives it and undoes that; its first test and console-pay-access.spec.ts now expect "הוספת תפקיד".
- Decided here, beyond the brief: a named role never holds pay by role (R0's screen already says its people get pay one by one on "מי רואה שכר"): no fallback to the base for a key an owner gives, no such row (`private.refuse_named_role_pay`), none copied, so a manager in a named role doesn't see pay just because managers do. Copying writes rows only where the source's rule differs from the base's, so a role copied from its base has none and follows its base wherever it hasn't been changed, consistent with "back to default" being the base's rule; keys owners don't switch (reservations, storefront) are never copied. `copy_role` beside `copy_from`, for copying the other built-in role (R0's sheet offers every role). `public.restore_business_role`, for the archive's undo (CLAUDE.md: undo over "are you sure"). Archiving clears the named role from memberships that left, so nobody comes back in an archived role. Rename's `role_name_en` null keeps the English name and empty removes it; the screens edit the Hebrew name only and show the English one in English. The twenty-role cap. The role writes check the role belongs to the business the screen was drawn for before calling the database.
- How R11b–d fit. R11b (giving one): `invites.named_role` with the same composite foreign key and the same live-role trigger as memberships' (`private.refuse_archived_named_role`'s rule: 22023, hint `archived`); `team_invite`, `team_invite_link`, `claim_invite` (set `named_role` from the invite, also on a rejoin, and refused in words for a role archived since the invite was sent, which the memberships trigger also refuses) and `set_member_role` take a named role, only a live one; the team screen offers named roles, and `GiveRole` on /manage/roles then shows for them too; the giving rules ask the named role's rules (`private.role_holds(…, named)` in `role_holds_beyond`; `role_holds_pay` is false for any named role, so only the rank and "holds what you don't" remain); `archive_business_role` adds open invites to "held"; `parseTeamInvite`, `invitableRoles` and `roleChoices` return `{ role, namedRole }`, and `RolePeopleRow.namedRole` already counts its people. R11c (view-as): `view_sessions.named_role`, and `my_permissions`, `businesses_holding` and `viewed_perms` pass it to `private.role_holds`; rank stays the base's. R11d (lists): `recipient_named_role` on lists, templates and schedules, with `list_recipient`, `holds_list` and `list_holders` matching `memberships.named_role`.
- After review: the owner's people are read under RLS again (`getRolePeopleForOwner` with `createClient()`, as R9's `getAccessPeople`: memberships' table-wide select and its member and view policies already return `named_role`), the console's with the secret key; Vitest covers both and the missing column. `private.refuse_archived_named_role` (a trigger, before insert or update of `named_role` on memberships, after the base trigger by name) refuses putting anyone in an archived role of the business (22023, hint `archived`), locking its row `for key share` so an archive under way is waited for; another business's role or base is still the foreign key's 23503. pgTAP (plan 53 unchanged, assertions extended): an archived role of the business itself is not renamed (P0002 `archived`), given rules (P0002), copied (P0002) or held (22023 `archived`), and its kept rows never become pay (22023 `pay`, the trigger's update path); the new function among the revoked and the security-definer ones (14). Screens: "נותנים את התפקיד לאנשים במסך הצוות" only for managers and workers, as the team screen gives no named role until R11b (e2e asserts none at each width); removing a named role puts the keyboard on the toast's "ביטול" (`focusAction`, as chat's removals), since its control goes with it; removing is a ghost `text-danger` button with its hint under it, outside the rename row, as the lists' "הסרת הרשימה", and on a computer the column head's is `text-danger` with the same hint; "ארכיון" is gone from the words: "הסרת התפקיד", "התפקיד {name} הוסר" (as the add's undo already said), "הוחזר" in the log; `failure.limit` takes `{max}` (`ROLE_LIMIT`, in `<ltr>`), and at the limit "הוספת תפקיד" is off with that line under the title. Not run here: the migration and every pgTAP file (no database), `pnpm build`, Playwright (roles.spec.ts, console-pay-access.spec.ts, design.spec.ts's twin), and the screens by eye at 390, 820 and 1440. database.types.ts is written by hand as the generator writes it.

## 2026-11-16 · chat · roles and permissions: the owner's own screen (R9)

- On chat/roles-owner-screen over R6 (PR #171) on main with R0–R5 and R7; R8 (chat open) merges before it. The roles plan's R9 with Orel's answers of 15 November 2026 on #1: only owners (and the ConceptApp team from the console) change permissions, every change saves at once with a StatusToast and "ביטול", pay stays on "מי רואה שכר" (a link, never a second switch: critique 20). No migration: the owner's read is the console's (`public.role_permission_matrix`, secret key only) after the session's person is checked to be the business's owner, and its answer says nothing about any person (the owner already reads the `role_permissions` rows it comes from), so no policy is widened and no new function is needed.
- `/manage/roles` (app/(app)/manage/roles/page.tsx, roles.ts, actions.ts, roles-data.ts, loading.tsx): R0's `RolesView` over the business's rules. Gate `rolesAccess()`, the same as /manage/access's `accessAccess` (`changesPermissions(role)` in @domain/role-access: owners only), read only in a view session (a view as the owner sees every switch off and the view line; it never writes), not found for everyone else, managers included at any level. Data: `getRoleMatrixForOwner` (new in @db/roles, the console's read under its own name and comment) and `getAccessPeople` (RLS, the business's active people with their photos, as "מי רואה שכר" lists them, by name), drawn by `rolesData` (moved from the console's roles-data.ts into the app, which now draws it too; the console wraps it with no photos): the areas whose module is on, each open once its keys are settable, managers and workers with their faces and counts, the fixed owners and accountants. ## 2026-11-16 · chat · roles and permissions: chat by its permissions (R8)

Nothing changes until someone switches: managers hold all three by default, workers none, owners always all.

## 2026-11-16 · chat · roles and permissions: the team by its permissions (R6)

- On chat/roles-team over main at 6ad2871 (R0 to R3 merged; R7, R4 and R5 to merge before it). The roles plan's R6 in its revised form (critique items 6, 14, 15 and 17), with Orel's answers of 15 November 2026 on #1: in the team a role is at edit, view or none; managers can be lowered and workers raised; only owners and the team change permissions; pay stays the owner's switch (Orel, 8 November). Nothing changes until someone switches, but one thing: managers hold team.view and team.edit by default, workers neither, owners always both.
- **A behaviour change, on purpose (critique 17, Orel's decision of 8 November):** someone other than an owner can no longer give a role that holds pay by role (a `role_permissions` row for `clock.payroll`), by an invite, a fresh link or a change of role (42501, hint `pay`). Until now, where an owner gave managers pay (the demo shop does), a manager inviting or promoting someone to manager passed pay on. Now only an owner can. An invite sent before (by a manager to the manager's role, before or after the owner gave it pay) isn't claimed either: `private.invite_closed` closes an invite to a role that holds pay that neither an owner nor the ConceptApp team sent (`claim_invite` answers P0002, the join screen reads it as expired), until an owner's fresh link or the team's makes it theirs. Where no role holds pay, nothing changes.
- Migration `20261115001300_roles_team.sql`.
